金融市場投資週報                 2026 年 9 月 21 日
上週重點訊息
◼ Fed 三年來首次升息，點陣圖顯示年底前再升一碼：Fed 於 9/16 一致決議升息 1 碼，將聯
邦基金利率目標區間提高至 3.75%～4.00%，為 2023 年 7 月以來首次升息。最新利率預測
中，18 名官員有 16 人預期年底前至少仍需再升息 1 碼。最新經濟預測顯示，Fed 上修今明
兩年實質 GDP 成長率並下修失業率預估值，但也預期通膨壓力將比原先估計更為頑強。主席
Warsh 並指出美國經濟、就業、內需與資本投資仍具韌性，金融環境難稱緊縮。

◼ AI 安全疑慮引發半導體股急跌，但科技產業內部仍呈現明顯分化： OpenAI、Anthropic 等
AI 業者高層呼籲放慢 AI 發展速度、強化安全防護，引發市場重新評估 AI 投資成長速度與資
本支出前景，拖累費城半導體指數 9/14 重挫約 5.9%，但軟體股反而上漲。現階段尚不足以
證明 AI 資本支出循環已反轉，資金亦未全面撤離科技產業，而是從估值較高、籌碼較擁擠的
AI 硬體轉向其他科技次產業。
◼ 日本央行決議升息 1 碼，符合市場預期：日本央行 9/18 以 7 比 2 決議將無擔保隔夜拆款利
率由 1.0%調升至 1.25%。此為該行 2024 年來第 6 次升息，符合市場預期，故升息後對風險
資產衝擊有限。BOJ 明言將「繼續升息並調整寬鬆程度」 ，並將審視調整的時點與步調。
市場展望與操作策略
◼ 科技股中期趨勢未見反轉，但短線波動料加劇：儘管 FOMC 9 月升息，點陣圖暗示今年可能
再升一碼，但其對經濟成長率與失業率預期仍朝正向發展，同時股市也止跌上揚，顯示投資

人認為企業獲利成長動能足以抵消利率上揚的負面影響，基本面較佳的科技股仍具資金承
接，惟宜留意股市短線仍可能因地緣政治與經濟數據表現而出現波動。
◼ 中天期美債及投資級債風險報酬相對均衡：Fed 重啟升息且點陣圖暗示年底前或再升息，短天
期公債殖利率直接受到政策利率上修影響 ； 天期過長的債券則持續面臨財政赤字 、 發債供給及
期限溢價上升壓力，利率敏感度偏高。中天期(5~10 年期)美國公債既可取得目前相對較高收
益，亦具適度存續期間，在未來通膨降溫時較有機會受惠於殖利率回落，風險報酬較為均衡。
投資級債亦具較高收益與信用防禦特性，有助平衡投資組合波動。

2026 年 10 月 15 日 全9 頁

Indicators Update 
2026 年 8 月機械受注 
製造業・非製造業（船電除く）のいずれも2 カ月ぶりに増加

経済調査部 エコノミスト 吉井 希祐

[要約] 
◼ 2026 年8 月の機械受注（船電除く民需）は前月比＋5.2％と2 カ月ぶりに増加し、コン
センサス（Bloomberg 調査：同＋2.3％）を上回った。民需（船電除く）を 3 カ月移動
平均で見ると同＋3.6％となり、内閣府は機械受注の基調判断を「持ち直しの動きがみ
られる」に据え置いた。 
◼ 製造業からの受注額は前月比＋1.6％と 2 カ月ぶりに増加した。非鉄金属やはん用・生
産用機械、その他輸送用機械などの業種からの受注が増加した。 非製造業（船電除く）
からの受注額は同＋2.9％と 2 カ月ぶりに増加した。リース業や卸売業・小売業、通信
業などの業種からの受注が増加した。 
◼ 先行きの民需（船電除く）は 緩やかな増加基調を辿るとみている。企業の設備投資意
欲は強く、人手不足対応のための省力化投資や、AI 関連投資などが引き続き期待され
よう。ただし、中東情勢のさらなる悪化に伴い 、受注が下振れするリスクには注意が
必要だ。事業環境の不確実性の高まりや原材料の価格高騰・供給不足に対する懸念か
ら、企業の設備投資への姿勢が慎重化する恐れがある。

【総括】船電除く民需は 2 カ月ぶりに増加 
2026 年 8 月の機械受注（船電除く民需）は前月比＋5.2％と 2 カ月ぶりに増加し、コンセン
サス（Bloomberg 調査：同＋2.3％）を上回った（図表2）。製造業は2 カ月ぶりに増加し、非製
造業（船電除く）も2 カ月ぶりに増加した（図表 3）。民需（船電除く）を3 カ月移動平均で見
ると同＋3.6％となり、内閣府は機械受注の基調判断を「持ち直しの動きがみられる」に据え
置いた。

【製造業】非鉄金属などが全体を押し上げたが、業種によりまちまちの結果 
2026 年 8 月の製造業からの受注額は前月比＋1.6％と 2 カ月ぶりに増加した。機種別に見る
と、原動機・重電機、輸送機械、電子・通信機械、工作機械が増加した一方、産業機械は減少
した（図表 4 左、大和総研による季節調整値） 。業種別では、17 業種中 8 業種が増加した。非
鉄金属（同＋132.6％）やはん用・生産用機械（同＋9.3％）、その他輸送用機械（同＋43.7％）
などの業種からの受注が増加した。他方、造船業（同▲ 41.0％）や電気機械（同▲5.9％）な
どからの受注は減少した。化学工業（同▲11.5％）では大型案件が 1 件あったものの、受注増
には至らなかった。

【非製造業】リース業や卸売業・小売業などを中心に増加 
2026 年8 月の非製造業（船電除く）からの受注額は前月比＋2.9％と2 カ月ぶりに増加した。
機種別に見ると、電子・通信機械、原動機・重電機が増加した一方、輸送機械、産業機械、工
作機械は減少した（図表 4 右、大和総研による季節調整値） 。業種別では、11 業種中 7 業種が
増加した。リース業（同＋136.0％）や卸売業・小売業（同＋29.2％）、通信業（同＋16.9％）
などの業種からの受注が増加した。他方、その他非製造業（同▲ 28.0％）や運輸業・郵便業
（同▲13.9％）などからの受注は減少した。

図表 4：業種別・機種別に見た機械受注額の動 き

除外したが、26 年8 月は前月比▲9.5％であった。

【外需】2 カ月連続で減少したものの依然高水準 
2026 年 8 月の外需は前月比▲7.7％と 2 カ月連続で減少した（図表 5） 。機種別では、原動
機・重電機、産業機械、電子・通信機械が減少した一方、工作機械、輸送機械は増加した（ 図
表 6、大和総研による季節調整値） 。外需は 2025 年中旬以降急増していたため、依然高水準で
あるが、増加をけん引してきた電子・通信機械の増勢にはこのところ一服感が見られる。輸送
機械で3 件、電子・通信機械で2 件、原動機・重電機で1 件の合計6 件の大型案件があった。 
機械受注の外需動向を地域別に見る上で参考になる工作機械受注を確認すると、8 月の外需
は前月比＋8.7％と3 カ月連続で増加した（日本工作機械工業会、大和総研による季節調整値、
図表 7） 。米国（同＋15.4％）からの受注は 3 カ月連続で増加した。航空・宇宙関連の大型案件
があったほか、AI 関連の受注が好調だったようだ1。欧州（EU＋英国、同＋16.9％）からの受
注も3 カ月連続で増加した。中国（同＋2.0％）からの受注は2 カ月ぶりに増加した（図表8）。 
工作機械受注は9 月分がすでに公表されており、内需は前月比▲3.3％と4 カ月ぶりに減少し
た。他方、外需は同＋2.8％と4 カ月連続で増加し、過去最高を更新し続けている。 
図表 5：一般機械の輸出と機械受注の外 需

【先行き】民需（船電除く）の緩やかな増加基調を見込むも中東情勢が下振れリスク 
先行きの民需（船電除く）は、緩やかな増加基調を辿るとみている。日銀短観の 9 月調査に
おける「生産・営業用設備判断 DI」 （先行き、全規模）を見ると、製造業（▲6％pt）と非製造
業（▲7％pt）のいずれでも、設備の不足感が示されている（図表 9） 。また、2026 年度の設備
投資計画（全規模、含む土地、ソフトウェアと研究開発投資額は含まない）は、製造業（前年
度比＋8.3％） 、非製造業（同＋7.3％）ともに堅調に推移している（図表 10）。企業の設備投資
意欲は強く、人手不足対応のための省力化投資や、AI 関連投資などが引き続き期待されよう。 
ただし、中東情勢のさらなる悪化に伴い、受注が下振れするリスクには注意が必要だ。ホル
ムズ海峡の通航が困難な状況が続く中、サウジアラビアとイエメンの親イラン武装組織フーシ
の間で攻撃の応酬が続くなど、エネルギー供給をめぐる先行き不透明感は強い。事業環境の不
確実性の高まりや原材料の価格高騰・供給不足に対する懸念から、企業の 設備投資への姿勢が
慎重化する恐れがある。 
図表 9：機械受注額と生産・営業用設備判断 DI（全規模）

（注 1）機械受注額は季節調整値。太線は 3 カ月移動平均。 
（注 2）生産・営業用設備判断 DI の直近値は先行き、それ以外は最近。 
（出所）内閣府、日本銀行統計より大和総研作成

Document 060: 2026年9月美国CPI数据点评：美国9月CPI：风险在油价的Higher for Longer

Report source: 东吴证券
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 7
Status: ok

### Page 1

证券研究报告·宏观报告·宏观点评

东吴证券研究所

1 / 7 
请务必阅读正文之后的免责声明部分 
宏观点评 20261015  
美国 9 月 CPI：风险在 油价的 Higher for 
Longer——2026 年 9 月美国 CPI 数据点评

2026 年 10 月 15 日

相关研究  
《A 股 9 月复盘与 10 月展望——缩
量调整中的结构性分化，与政策托底
的十字路口》 
2026-10-14 
《 9 月美国 CPI 前瞻： 核心环比预计
温和，仍需关注油价扰动 ——海外周
报 20261011》 
2026-10-11

[Table_Tag]    [Table_Summary] 
◼ 核心观点：9 月美国 CPI 全面超预期，核心项目是超预期的主要来源。
与 8 月少数项目的扰动相比，9 月核心商品与服务环比的反弹具有广泛
性，通胀扩散度显著走高，超级核心通胀 3 个月均年率升至 4%以上的
高位，且二手车、 房租等分项近期走势与领先指数 “脱钩” 。这一通胀黏
性背后的必要条件是美国经济增长的韧性超预期，充分条件则是油价
“Higher for Longer”的现实强化了通胀的传导。展望看，若油价持续在
高位反复， 则高油价给明年通胀带来的基数效应利好的意义将减弱。货
币政策方面，10 月 28 日美联储加息与否对 11 月 3 日的美国中期选举
尤其重要， 因此虽然地缘事件的不确定性很高， 但我们更担忧伊朗在 10
月下旬升级冲突、给特朗普施压的可能性，这将强化美联储 10 月加息
预期走高的风险。就策略而言，中期选举仍是一个重要的事件节点，中
选后各方暂时消停、 美国国会格局清晰，市场的不确定性才会消除；我
们维持此前观点：10 月的不确定性高， 对风险偏好构成持续压制， 更适
合 11 月 3 日中选结果公布后进行右侧交易。 
◼ 美国 9 月 CPI：全面超预期， 加息预期升温。美国 9 月 CPI 环比+0.65%，
预期+0.6%， 前值+0.4%； 核心CPI 环比+0.33%， 预期+0.2%， 前值+0.29%。
CPI 同比+3.74%，预期+3.6%，前值+3.40%；核心 CPI 同比+2.56%，预
期+2.5%，前值+2.45%。由于总体 CPI 环比基本符合预期，核心项目的
超预期是 9 月通胀全面超预期的主要驱动，且核心 CPI 环比是在 8 月
“异常”的高基数上延续高于预期，带动核心 CPI 环比 3 个月均值显
著走高。 数据发布后，紧货币交易强化，交易员加息定价10 月 28 日美
联储加息概率从 20%左右升至最高 50%以上，全年加息预期最高升至约
1.5 次，美股和黄金下跌，美债收益率和美元上涨。随后，数位美联储
官员发言仍释放鸽派信号，表示不急于加息，10 月加息预期回落至约
30%，美债利率回落。 
◼ 通胀结构：核心分项环比全面走高，通胀广度扩散。总体来看，核心商
品、 居住服务、 除居住核心服务等核心CPI 大类在 9 月均环比反弹， 显
示通胀黏性仍大。①核心商品方面，9 月核心商品 CPI 环比+0.25%，为
2025 年 2 月以来最高， 前值+0.11%；结构上， 全球供应链压力、 关税和
AI 相关商品涨价均是贡献来源。其中，高占比的二手车价格拉动 9.6bps，
是核心商品环比上涨的主要贡献，且与其领先指标 Manheim 二手车走
势脱钩。与关税相关的休闲商品与服装， 二者环比分别由上月的+0.02%、
0%反弹至+0.31%、+0.86%，其中休闲商品中的玩具环比涨幅突出，拉
动核心 CPI 环比 6.6bps。②居住通胀方面，9 月居住通胀环比+0.39%，
较前值 +0.26%显著 反弹， 其 中租 金和 自住 房折算 环 比分别 反弹 至
+0.25%、+0.35%的较高中枢， 酒店住宿环比保持在+2.04%的高位。从领
先指标——领先约 16 个月的房价和领先约 6—8 个月的租金来看，居住
通胀应当仍有一定的下行空间，其在本月的反弹略超预期。③超级核心
通胀方面，由于上月的电信服务等一次性扰动的消退，9 月非居住核心
服务通胀环比由前值+0.42%回落至+0.34%， 超级核心通胀 （包括居住服
务中的酒店住宿） 环比由前值+0.51%回落至+0.42%，但环比均处于较高
中枢，且 3 个月均年率分别升至 4.43%、4.48%的高位。尤其是，从结
构来看，交运、休闲服务、酒店等更广泛的项目出现了上涨，超级核心
通胀结构所体现的通胀黏性更加突出。④通胀扩散度方面，反映通胀广
度的通胀扩散指数在 9 月显著走高，环比折年增速大于过去 5 年均值的
项目占比从 17.6%提升至 24.76%，环比折年增速大于 4%的项目占比从
20%提升至 35%。 
◼ 展望与策略启示： 风险在于油价的“higher for longer”：总体来看，9 月
美国 CPI 全面超预期， 其中核心项目是超预期的主要来源。 与8 月少数
项目的扰动相比，9 月核心商品与核心服务环比的反弹具有广泛性，通
胀扩散度显著走高， 我们认为其背后的必要条件是美国经济增长的韧性

### Page 2

2 / 7 
超预期，充分条件则是油价“Higher for Longer”的现实强化了通胀的
传导， 这对美联储来说是一个不容乐观的信号。展望看，①通胀趋势方
面，若油价持续在高位反复，则我们此前看好的“高油价带来的基数效
应”意义减弱：一来是油价的回落幅度有限， 产生的基数效应更加有限；
二来是高油价的持续意味着通胀更大的扩散风险。②货币政策方面，对
于特朗普而言，10 月 28 日美联储加息与否，对 11 月 3 日的美国中期
选举尤其重要，因此，虽然地缘事件的不确定性很高，但我们更担忧上
行风险，即伊朗在中期选举前的 10 月下旬主动挑高油价、给特朗普政
府施压的可能性，这将强化美联储 10 月加息预期走高的风险。就策略
启示而言， 我们强调在此前报告中的观点， 美国中期选举仍然是一个重
要的事件节点，中选后美伊双方暂时消停、美国国会格局清晰， 市场的
不确定性才会逐步消除；10 月的不确定性高，对风险偏好构成持续压
制，因此更适合 11 月 3 日中选结果公布后进行右侧交易。 
◼ 风险提示：油价失控推高通胀； 美联储维持高利率水平时间过长， 引发
金融系统流动性危机。

东吴证券投资评级标准 
投资评级基于分析师对报告发布日后 6 至 12 个月内行业或公司回报潜力相对基准表现
的预期（A 股市场基准为沪深 300 指数，香港市场基准为恒生指数，美国市场基准为标普 
500 指数， 新三板基准指数为三板成指 （针对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针对做市转
让标的），北交所基准指数为北证 50 指数），具体如下： 
公司投资评级： 
买入：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准在 15%以上； 
增持：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于 5%与 15%之间； 
中性：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于-5%与 5%之间； 
减持：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于-15%与-5%之间； 
卖出：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准在-15%以下。 
行业投资评级： 
增持： 预期未来 6 个月内，行业指数相对强于基准 5%以上； 
中性： 预期未来 6 个月内，行业指数相对基准-5%与 5%； 
减持： 预期未来 6 个月内，行业指数相对弱于基准 5%以上。 
我们在此提醒您， 不同证券研究机构采用不同的评级术语及评级标准。 我们采用的是相
对评级体系， 表示投资的相对比重建议。 投资者买入或者卖出证券的决定应当充分考虑自身
特定状况， 如具体投资目的、 财务状况以及特定需求等， 并完整理解和使用本报告内容， 不
应视本报告为做出投资决策的唯一因素。

Technology - Asia-Pacific (H/A)

CCL for PCB: spec upgrades well on track 
toward CY27/28 with pricing upside

Price Objective Change

Accessible version

### Page 2

CCL for PCB: spec upgrades well on track
toward CY27/28 with pricing upside
Migration in raw materials of CCL
Spec advancements in resin more debatable
Major raw materials for building CCL (copper clad laminate) include cloth, resin, and
copper foil. Glass cloth serves as grounding and offers support to the CCL and PCB
(printed circuit board) structure, and it is immersed in resins built on different in-house
chemical recipes by CCL makers to obtain PP (prepreg). Afterwards, CCL makers

add/laminate the copper foil on this intermediate product (PP), to form the CCL supplied
to PCB customers. Across the previous generations (Exhibit 2), we have seen rising
penetration of PPE/PPO (polyphenylene ether/oxide) gradually replacing FR-4/ epoxy
resins throughout M4-M8 grade CCL, while hydrocarbon resin gradually gains traction
from M8-grade CCL. Yet, there is a debate on PTFE-based (polytetrafluoroethylene) CCL
vs hydrocarbon resin as the CCL grade advances into M9/M10.

As illustrated in Exhibit 4, we see a more diversified path of resin upgrades from M8-
grade CCL, while the advancement in copper foil and glass cloth seems to be more
certain. In general, we note that the mix of PPO vs hydrocarbon used is roughly 2:1 in
M8-grade CCL, and such proportion reverses to 1:2 in M9-grade CCL. Yet, the broader
range of resin options could potentially alter the competitive dynamics within the CCL
industry, since different vendors are promoting (or have edge) in different types of CCL

solutions. Or in other words, the likely penetration by PTFE-based solutions (pushed
mainly by NVIDIA) will favor the suppliers with experiences in handling relevant products
(though smaller market size such as base station), while, at the same time, it might
curtail the headroom for major Taiwanese suppliers, including EMC and TUC (Taiwan
Union), to grow.
PPO/BMI vs hydrocarbon in M8/M9; hydrocarbon vs PTFE in M9/M10
In our view, hydrocarbon resin exhibits an ultra-low dielectric constant (Dk) of 2.2-2.6

and a dissipation factor (Df) reaching 0.001-0.005, as low as 0.0005 at high frequencies
(e.g., 10 GHz and above), thanks to its non-polar molecular backbone. However, there are
drawbacks to hydrocarbon resin, including rather limited flexibility and high brittleness
after curing, which necessitate the mix with ductile polymers such as PPO or BMI
(bismaleimide) to improve the toughness (peel strength). Nonetheless, the performance
in Dk/Df would be reduced after adding PPO/BMI to hydrocarbon resin, which makes the

solution not that favorable in meeting customers’ demand/requirement.
Within hydrocarbon resin, we note there are two major advanced versions, including ODV
(oxidative vulcanization; lower-end) and BCB (benzocyclobutene; higher-end), and key
suppliers for the latter include Dow Chemical and Asahi Kasei. In terms of Df, <0.0008
can be reached by BCB type of hydrocarbon resin based on the standard of M9-grade
CCL, and the number would hit <0.0005 based on the standard of M9+/M10-grade. By

CCL suppliers, we note EMC (Elite Material) adopts hydrocarbon/BMI resin in M9-grade
CCL, while Panasonic blends PPO or BMI (with hydrocarbon) in M9 (vs PPO/hydrocarbon
in M7/M8). Shengyi Technology also adopts PPO resin in M9-grade offering.
Given the disadvantages (discussed above) of PPO resin, some CCL providers
alternatively also adopt mPPO (modified PPO; Exhibit 5) by blending the PPO with other
resins (e.g., epoxy methacrylate, styrene). By doing so, the overall dielectric and thermal

properties of the composite are enhanced, and the reduced molecular weight improves
solubility in solvents like toluene and MEK (methyl ethyl ketone). This in turn enables the
resin to thermoset, and it becomes more suitable for CCL processing. By application, we
believe it is adopted in the LPU (language processing unit) and UBB (universal
baseboard) under Vera Rubin platform, 1.6T networking switch, and will also be
potentially (competing with PTFE-based solutions) adopted in the backplane.

### Page 3

Similar to what we have seen in PPO/PPE vs hydrocarbon in M8/M9, we believe
hydrocarbon and PTFE will coexist and complement each other in M9/M10-grade CCL,
while the sheer adoption on one side is unlikely, given 1) different consideration on
cost/performance by different customers; and 2) the difficulty in sourcing across resin
and the adjacent equipment in producing different types of CCL (e.g. vacuum hot press
machine). Meanwhile, we note that PPO/PPE cannot be paired with PTFE given the

conflicts in characteristics.
In addition to the specialized plasma treatment, and long lead time in pressing machine
(operating under high temperature) with high costs, one of the other major drawbacks to
PTFE is the lack of stiffness in texture which makes it hard to be processed under PCB
drilling/lamination. Thus, SiO2 (silicon dioxide) is used as a filler (or with increasing
consumption vs that in M7/M8-grade CCL) to fill the resin matrix, and this helps reduce

the thermal expansion coefficient (CTE) and enhance the mechanical strength of the
composites. Elsewhere, a lack of adhesion to copper foil is the other key technological
challenge when processing PTFE-based solutions, and this requires the help from
hydrocarbon resin (to enhance the adhesion).
In short, the pursuit of more advanced materials like PTFE/hydrocarbon will
simultaneously incur other technological challenges, which, in our view, makes relevant

solutions to become more versatile (to solve the associated challenges). In Exhibit 6, we
present the promoted solutions of M9/M10-grade CCL in the market, and we compare
different metrics, including signal loss, mechanical strength, processibility, and costs.
Given the low penetration by M9-grade CCL currently (evidenced by single-digit% of
industry leader EMC’s revenue), we believe none of these solutions has become
mainstream. In other words, we deem the ongoing development and qualification/testing

results worth monitoring, and these include the feedback from board, ODM (original
design manufacturer) and end-customers’ levels. In our view, the most imminent (and
perhaps critical) one will likely be the solution adopted by Rubin Ultra switch board and
backplane (if any), which could be finally confirmed by end-2026/early-2027.

Exhibit 5: A comparison between PPO and mPPO 
mPPO carries enhanced processability and reactivity when compared with PPO 
Characteristics/type of 
resin PPO mPPO 
Dk Inherently low. Values for PPO-based composites are typically in the range of 2.1 - 3.5.  Retains low Dk, with high-performance grades achieving tightly controlled values, e.g., 2.8 - 3.0 for M9 grade. 
Df Inherently very low. Values for PPO-based systems can be as low as 0.0006. Significantly reduced and optimized. Ranges from 0.004 – 0.008 for M6/M7 grades down to <0.002 (typically 0.0015–0.002) for ultra-low loss M9 grades. 
Processability Limited. High melt viscosity and lack of reactivity make it difficult to process.  Better. Low molecular weight and reactive end-groups enable good solubility, compatibility with CCL manufacturing, and thermosetting cross-linking. 
Reactivity Low. Lacks sufficient reactive groups to cross-link effectively with other resins. High. Functionalized end-groups (e.g., styrene, methacrylate) allow it to chemically bond with other components in the resin formulation.

Clearer upgrades in glass cloth and copper foil  
While the upgrades in resin system appear to be more debatable with a versatility in 
solutions, we witness a firmer roadmap advancement in glass cloth and copper foil. For 
glass cloth, (see reports) Nittobo shared the view of 100% demand growth in NER this 
year (FY3/27), followed by another c60% growth next year. As for copper foil, Mitsui 
Kinzoku also revised up the mix of HVLP4 and above products in 1H26 (within very 
smooth profile copper foil) to 45% (was 40%), and the figure is expected to reach 60% 
in 2H26. On pricing, we note that Mitsui Kinzoku also mentioned it would try pursuing a 
higher ASP in product segments where demand remains strong, while there is a higher 
processing fee (Exhibit 7) charged by copper foil suppliers in the high-end copper foil 
market. All-in-all, we see positive indications to the high-end CCL pricing, given that the 
CCL makers will try to pass through the inflated costs (due to stronger demand and the 
premium in processing fee) to the product ASP.

Ongoing spec migration on multiple fronts into CY27-28 
We see a clear upgrade cycle for CCL materials and PCB production technique across 
networking switches and AI (artificial intelligence) accelerators, including GPUs (graphic 
processing unit) and ASICs (application specific integrated circuits). In our view, the spec 
advancement (Exhibit 8) is primarily driven by faster data rates and increasingly 
stringent signal-integrity requirements. For NVIDIA, GB200/GB300 largely adopt M8-
grade CCL, with low-Dk1/2 glass cloth and HVLP2/3 copper foil. The transition to VR200 
drives further CCL specification upgrades toward M8+/M9-grade, along with the 
adoption of HVLP4 copper foil in VR200 compute board and midplane, while LPU adopts 
Q cloth with less signal loss (vs low Dk2).  
While NVIDIA tends to place greater emphasis on glass cloth upgrades, ASICs and 
networking switches are more focused on copper foil migration. Among ASICs, we 
expect the CCL spec for Google TPU (tensor processing unit) v8 to migrate to M8+

based on low Dk2 cloth and HVLP3 copper foil (vs M7-grade CCL with low Dk1 and
HVLP2 for TPU v7), followed by M9-grade adoption in TPU v9 based on HVLP4 copper
foil (with either low Dk2 or Q-cloth). As for the Trainium chips by Amazon, there is a
more visible upgrade in copper foil (vs glass cloth) taking place in Trainium 3 (2026),
while its OAM (open accelerator module) in Trainium 4 (2027) will likely be upgraded to
M8-grade CCL based on low Dk2 cloth. Simultaneously, we also expect a migration

toward HDI (high-density interconnect) from HLC (high-layer count), with regard to the
PCB production for the UBB (universal baseboard) in TPU v9 and Trainium 4.

Along with the data-rate migration toward 800G and beyond (from 100/200G) for optical
module, PCB structure evolves from conventional tenting design toward more
complicated mSAP/SAP (semi-additive process) with higher layer count, enabling finer
L/S (line/space) and smaller pitch (Exhibit 9). To elaborate, L/S of 3.2T optical module
should shrink materially to 15/15μm from 60/60μm at 800G, and it was 125/125um for

100G generation. Simultaneously, the layer count would also increase to up to layers for
3.2T products, vs 10-layer in 400G generation.
Amid the backdrop of rapid spec migration, major PCB suppliers are accelerating
capacity expansion to capture the burgeoning demand in high-end optical module
(Exhibit 10), and these include Avary under ZDT (Zhen Ding Technology), Founder, SCC
(Shennan Circuits), Unimicron and Compeq. While Unimicron remains the biggest

supplier of optical module PCB (printed circuit board) in 2H26, we flag Avary and SCC
could potentially become the other major suppliers in 2027, given their more aggressive
capacity increase.

Expect further ASP uplift driven by mix & cost inflation 
We expect CCL (for PCB) names’ revenue growth to be fueled by continuous capacity 
expansion and ASP uplifts, and these include EMC, TUC, Shengyi Tech and Kingboard 
Laminates among our coverage. The ASP increase, in our view, will be driven by both 
price hike on a like-for-like basis (given the cost push) and mix improvements to higher 
CCL grades, and we note the former/latter represent bigger boost to the near-
term/long-term ASP. To elaborate, the difference in near-term vs long-term boost can 
be explained by 1) a shorter production cycle time of less than 1-2 weeks by CCL makers 
which normally carry less than a quarter of inventory; and 2) a continuous spec 
advancement in CCL material grade given the requirement in data-transmission.  
In Exhibit 11-13, we decipher the revenue growth by different CCL names based on the 
planned capacity expansion and ASP uplift, as well as the assumption of 95%/90%/90% 
capacity utilization rate by EMC/TUC/Shengyi Tech in 2028. In our view, the implied ASP 
growth of 4%/11%/18% by respective names look conservative, and we see room for 
further upside in the case of continuous cost inflation from the raw materials (e.g. glass 
cloth, copper foil). Further, we believe a smaller magnitude of ASP increase by EMC (vs 
TUC/Shengyi Tech) in 2028 can be explained by its already significant exposure to high-
end CCL market in 2027. For reference, we present the CCL ASP by each grade with the 
figure for M4-grade indexed to 100 in Exhibit 20, and the gap of ASP by each grade 
remains quite meaningful, in our view.  
Regarding the CCL mix by grade, we break down the revenue contribution by different 
grades to each company throughout 2025-28, and we compare the numbers to their 
gross margins in Exhibit 14-19. When comparing the gross margin, we carve out the 
revenue mix from M7-grade and above CCL, given that M6-grade is already in supply-
demand balance (or slightly oversupply) situation from late-2025.

### Page 8

Among the three names, we expect EMC to remain the industry leader with 
73%/85%/92% revenue mix from M7-grade and above CCL in 2026/27/28, which helps 
strengthen its gross margin to 35.8%/36.7% in 2027/28 (vs 34% in 2026). TUC will 
follow suit with 43%/47%/53% revenue mix from M7-grade and above CCL in 
2026/27/28, with gross margin edging toward 32.6%/33.7% in 2027/28 (vs 30.4% in 
2026). As for Shengyi Tech, we expect 28%/35%/57% revenue mix from M7-grade and 
above CCL in 2026/27/28, and its gross margin (in CCL business) is expected to inch up 
to 31%/31.8 in 2027/28 (vs 30.7% in 2026).

 BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH 
Regarding the ASP uplifts by firm in 2027, we further decode into the factors of cost 
inflation and mix enhancement as shown in Exhibit 21. To simply put, we expect a 
smaller boost to EMC (vs TUC/Shengyi Tech) from cost inflation of raw materials and 
the corresponding pass-through, given its relatively lower revenue exposure to low-/mid-
end CCL market, where CCL makers pass through the cost more frequently upon 
squeezed margin profile. Yet, on the other hand, EMC (in terms of ASP) is expected to 
benefit more from the enhanced mix in 2027, thanks to the further ramp of M8/M8.5-
grade CCL as well as the penetration increase of M9 and PTFE-based solutions.  
Year-to-date, we believe there is already 40%+/20%+ price hike across low-/high-end 
CCL, and we do not rule out the likelihood of further price increase given the (see report) 
expectations of continuously rising copper price into late-2026 and 2027 (Exhibit 22). 
There was a price hike of VSP (very smooth profile) foil, effective from July 2026 by 
Mitsui Kinzoku given the demand strength and likely to be followed suit by a Taiwanese 
supplier (Co-Tech), based on our checks with supply chain. On the glass cloth side, we 
note the potential pricing upside from low Dk2 cloth given the supply shortage, while the 
price of low-end E-glass cloth (such as 7628 type) remains on the uptrend (Exhibit 23) 
and may reach CNY15-16 (per msq) by end-2026. In our view, all of these point to a 
benign pricing outlook for CCL makers.  
To pinpoint the benefits from price increase by CCL makers, we conduct a scenario 
analysis to each name’s 2027 bottom-line as shown in Exhibit 24. In short, we expect 
1.2-1.6% upside to the net income estimates coming from every 1ppt price hike, if such 
price increase purely reflects cost inflation. On the other hand, we expect 3.3-5.1% 
upside to the net income estimates, if the price hike is purely driven a supply shortage 
(which enables CCL makers to raise the ASP proactively). Further on Shengyi Tech, we 
assume that its PCB business (Shengyi Electronics) can also pass on the cost increase 
(from CCL side), with same magnitude to its ASP.  
For Kingboard Laminates, its vertical integration significantly enhances its earnings 
sensitivity to price hikes, as most of its key raw materials are produced in-house. 
Notably, price hikes in glass fiber cloth can translate almost entirely into profit, given in-
house production of yarn. Our analysis suggests that 100% increase in CCL prices could 
drive c.28ppt gross margin expansion for KBL, and more than 200% growth at the EPS 
level in 2026. This assumes that future price hikes are primarily driven by cloth 
tightness, while input costs for copper foil and resin remain relatively stable.

Sector view: stay positive on industry leaders  
We stay constructive on the CCL for PCB area, given the ongoing spec upgrades and 
robust demand growth into 2027-28, and we believe the worries from CPO (co-packaged 
optics) proliferation are either overstated or distant, as the mass adoption of CPO to 
replace NPO (near-packaged optics) or pluggable optical modules will not take place until 
2029/30, at earliest.  
Currently, we see extended lead-time (of production) toward 2-3 months by high-end 
CCL makers in 2H26 (vs 1-2 months seen in 1H26), while low/mid-end suppliers’ lead 
time is also extended to 1-2 months (7-10 days before). We also believe the current 
valuation by EMC (21-22x 2027E P/E) and TUC (17-18x 2027E P/E) looks compelling, 
given that the numbers do not seem lofty when compared with the historical trading 
range of 1) 15-20x before mid-2023; and 2) normally above 20x after mid-2023 when 
the strong demand from AI server starts to emerge. As for Kingboard Laminates, it is 
traded at 8x 2027E P/E, below historical average of 10x. 
Among our coverage, we highlight Buy-rated EMC, given its technology leadership in 
high-end CCL area along with diversified customer profile across GPU and ASIC 
accounts, as well as TUC, given its (see report) aggressive capacity expansion into 2027 
and beyond. We also highlight Buy rating on Doosan Corp and Kingboard Laminates, 
given the former’s solid position in NVIDIA GPU and Amazon switch, as well as the 
latter’s robust earnings expansion amid CCL price hike and rising high-end contribution 
starting 2027. As for Shengyi Tech, we remain relatively conservative, given its relatively 
slower expansion in CCL capacity along with a milder growth in the PCB business.

### Page 14

EMC (2383 TT; Buy; PO NT$8,200) 
We reckon EMC’s potential share loss in Rubin Ultra switch board (tray), given the 
competition in PTFE-based (hybrid) solutions (vs M9-Q), as well as the ongoing debate 
between PTFE and quartz-glass/hydrocarbon resin solutions into M10-grade CCL. We 
also note that M9-Q (where EMC has more notable technology leadership) is likely to be 
adopted in backplane by NVIDIA, no matter if it will initially be seen in Rubin Ultra or 
Feynman generation. Net-net, we believe the impact on bottom-line (if any) from 
aforementioned headwinds should be confined to LSD (low-single-digit)%, considering 
1) 80-90k (per month) of relevant demand by NVIDIA’s switch board vs EMC’s capacity 
of ~6/9mn in 2026/27; and 2) the demand backfill from other high-end projects across 
AMD and other ASIC customers.  
Looking ahead, we see further expansion plan beyond 1H28 as a catalyst, if it is 
announced. In addition to the CCL for PCB business, we note that substrate CCL 
business boasts at least 50-60% GM (gross margin), and EMC could expand relevant 
capacity to 1.2mn sheet /month which could help expedite its market-share gain toward 
~5% (vs 1-2% in 2025). We raise 2026/27/28E EPS by 5%/11%/14% after penciling in 
stronger revenue growth and gross margin profile, while our PO stays at NT$8,200 as 
we apply a lower P/E multiple of 30x (was 34x), with the same valuation period of 2H27-
1H28E, mainly reflecting the overhang from competition in PTFE-based solutions.

TUC (6274 TT; Buy; PO NT$2,600) 
Looking into 2027, we flag multiple positives from spec advancements for TUC, 
including 1) the potentially partial adoption of M9-grade CCL by 1.6T networking switch, 
where TUC’s solution is based on low Dk3 glass cloth; 2) the upgrade to M8-grade CCL 
for LEO (low earth orbit); and 3) the migration toward M8-grade material by Vera CPU 
(central processing unit). Meanwhile, we share the view that hybrid solution from PTFE-
based CCL may last toward M10-grade. Demand from high-end optical module (800G 
and above) could have remained underestimated at current stage, as we believe relevant 
TAM (total addressable market) for CCL could reach NT$30bn+, which represents close 
to 50% of TUC’s total revenue in 2026. In terms of Thailand operations, we believe it 
could turn breakeven or profitable from 3Q26, thanks to the smooth ramp of 
capacity/output to pair with the robust demand growth outlook from customers.  
We raise 2026/27/28E EPS by 7%/11%/7% after baking in the assumptions of stronger 
revenue growth and slightly enhanced GM profile as well as operating leverage. Our PO 
stays at NT$2,600, based on a lower P/E multiple of 27x (was 30.5x) and an unchanged 
valuation period of 2H27-1H28E. We attribute the cut in valuation multiple to the likely 
growing traction by PTFE-based solutions that serve as other alternatives in the high-
end CCL market.

Shengyi Technology (600183 CH; U/P; PO CNY99) 
We believe revenue contribution from PTFE-based CCL could reach ~10% based on the 
assumption of US$300-400 ASP per piece, and we expect the ramp of such business to 
bolster the overall margin profile thanks to ~50% GM by relevant solutions. Yet we also 
note that the firm’s capacity for M6-grade and above CCL remains relatively limited at 
~10% mix currently, and further capacity ramp will be dependent on the progress in 
Thailand and Songshan Lake (for M7-M9), after its Jiangxi phase 2 capacity became fully 
loaded after 1H26.  
We raise 2026/27/28E EPS by 29%/21%/18% after baking in 2Q26 results and the 
assumptions of stronger revenue growth and margin profiles, which also reflect its 
further share gain in NVIDIA’s AI server system/platforms. Our PO goes to CNY99 (from 
CNY83), same based on 24x 2027E P/E, and the volatility risk rating is changed to C 
from B.

Valuation discussion  
Both Buy-rated EMC and TUC are expected to post stronger EPS growth toward 2028 
along with a higher dividend yield. However, they trade at lower P/E multiples when 
compared with peers’ average in CCL industry. As for P/B multiples, the premium 
valuation by these two names, in our view, can be explained by much higher ROE 
expected in 2027-28. On the other hand, our more conservative view on Shengyi can be 
supported by its more stretched valuation (for both P/E and P/B) vs milder EPS growth 
and lower dividend yield, when compared with the peers’ average. Overall, we believe the 
(see report) overhang from CPO proliferation is already largely reflected in the valuation 
of CCL names, which we believe also renders an attractive opportunity for EMC/TUC, 
considering their solid earnings growth ahead fueled by continuous mix upgrades and 
more aggressive capacity expansion.

A list of acronyms on front page 
CCL – copper clad laminate  
PCB – printed circuit board  
S/D – supply/demand  
CPO – co-packaged optics  
EMC – Elite Material  
TUC – Taiwan Union 
PTFE – polytetrafluoroethylene 
GPU – graphic processing unit 
HC - hydrocarbon 
PPO/PPE - polyphenylene ether/oxide 
SiO2 - silicon dioxide 
AI – artificial intelligence  
GPU – graphic processing unit  
ASIC – application specific integrated circuit  
HDI – high density interconnect  
GM – gross margin

Price objective basis & risk 
Doosan Corp (DOSAF) 
Our PO of W1,800,000 is derived from SOTP valuation, which implies a fair market cap 
of W32tn = W20tn for core businesses (based on 20x 2027-28E P/E, which is in line with

### Page 22

the average of CCL peers) + W12tn in listed equity investments (Doosan Enerbility, 
Doosan Robotics, and Oricom) + net debt (breakeven) + other investments (W0.25tn, 
mostly non-listed affiliates). Our W1,800,000 PO implies 8x 2027-28E P/B, which 
appears much higher than the 2023-25 average of 4x, as well as a relatively high P/E 
(31x based on 2027-28E EPS) versus other Korea Holdco companies (5-10x). Key reason 
behind our relatively higher fair multiples compared to its historical average and its 
peers should be Doosan's rapidly growing CCL business thanks to its dominant market 
share position within NVIDIA.

Upside risks: (1) further market share gains in high-end CCL for major US hyperscalers, 
(2) a more favorable CCL pricing environment driven by sustained demand growth and 
tight industry supply, (3) incremental demand opportunities from emerging technologies 
such as co-packaged optics (CPO) and other networking equipment, and (4) faster-than-
expected capacity ramp-up.

Downside risks: (1) a slowdown in AI infrastructure spending or cuts to US hyperscaler 
capex, which could weaken demand for high-end CCL used in AI servers and networking 
equipment, (2) intensifying competition and pricing pressure in the advanced CCL 
market, and (3) potential delays in Doosan's CCL capacity expansion. 
Elite Material (ETMCF) 
Our PO of NT$8,200 is based on 30x 2H27-1H28E P/E, at the mid-to-high-end of its 
historical range of 6-34x since 2016. In our view, the multiple is appropriate, given the 
market-share expansion in server/networking, where we see structural growth in 
addressable market.

Upside risks are (1) share gain at substrate CCL, (2) stronger 5G smartphone shipment, 
(3) greater 5G smartphone spec upgrades, and (4) favorable FX and raw material cost 
change.

Downside risks are (1) share loss at iPhone, (2) keener competition in high-end CCL, and 
(3) unfavorable FX and raw material cost change. 
Kingboard Laminates (KGBLF) 
We set our PO at HK$65, on 13x 2H26-1H27E P/E. 13x is around +0.5SD of KBL's 
historical trading range, and matches with its last earnings upcycle in 2021 (led by e-
glass price hike) while also reflects rising mix from high-end AI-related deliveries.

Downside risks: (1) slower-than-expected CCL price hike, (2) better-than-expected 
industry supply of upstream materials esp. cloth, (3) slower-than-expected AI server 
demand, and (4) slower-than-expected external glass fiber cloth sales.

Upside risks: (1) stronger-than-expected CCL price hike, (2) slower-than-expected 
industry supply of upstream materials esp. cloth, (3) stronger-than-expected AI server 
demand, and (4) faster-than-expected external glass fiber cloth sales. 
Shengyi (DGUNF) 
Our PO of CNY99 is based on 24x 2027E P/E, close to the mid-to-high-end range of the 
historical average of 10-35x in 2017-2024. In our view, such valuation multiple is backed 
by operating margin and ROE of 23% and 37% in 2026-28E (vs 11% and 12% in 2023-
25).

Upside risks to our price objective are: (1) better macro conditions, (2) faster 5G 
deployment, (3) less-intensive competition, and (4) favorable FX and raw material cost 
changes.

### Page 23

Downside risks are: (1) worse macro conditions, (2) slower 5G deployment, (3) more 
severe competition, and (4) unfavorable FX and raw material cost changes. 
Taiwan Union Technology Corporation (TWUNF) 
Our PO NT$2,600 is based on 27x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is at the mid-to-high-end of 
the historical trading range of 8-31x during 2016-2026. In our view, the valuation 
multiple is justified, given operating margin and ROE expansion toward 26% and 63% in 
2026-28E (vs 13% and 16% in 2023-25) along with robust free-cash flow generation in 
over the same period.

Downside risks are 
1) Fluctuation in raw material prices, which could lead to a more volatile gross margin 
for TUC in the near term 
2) More intensified competition in CCL, which could pressure TUC's overall margin 
profile 
3) Adoption of new type of upstream material (e.g., quartz), which could bring business 
uncertainty for the names in CCL industry, including TUC 
4) Worse-than-expected execution by the company, which could cause market-share loss 
and/or decreased business traction with customers

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 -  Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment rating 
of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, the 0-
12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a stock 
should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst ’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation).

INCOME RATINGS, indicators of potential cash dividends, are: 7 - same/higher (dividend considered to be secure), 8 - same/lower (dividend not considered to be secure)

region or other classification(s). A stock ’s coverage cluster is included in the most recent BofA Global Research report referencing the stock.

Chroma Ate Inc.  | Asia Pacific
September  Sales  +22% MoM/
+206% YoY
In this report, we focus on Chroma's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for share price movement.
Details:
• September revenue was NT$6,860mn (22% MoM/+206% YoY), a record
high.
• 3Q26 revenue came in at  NT$17,926mn (+32% QoQ/+180% YoY),
significantly higher than  our estimate at NT$14,772mn (+9% QoQ/+130%
YoY) and consensus at NT$14,555mn (+8% QoQ/+127% YoY).

Our view:
• We believe that the strength in 3Q26 was mainly driven by the
semiconductor and photonics business, especially SL T, metrology,
transceiver/CPO testing, etc.
• We think that Chroma might start to ship FT handlers and ovens in small
volume from late 2026/early 2027, which represent new business
opportunities for the coming years.
• Stay OW.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Chroma Ate Inc. (2360.TW)
Base case, 45x 2027e P/E. We use P/E as our valuation methodology because we think it better
captures Chroma's earnings growth momentum. The stock traded at an average valuation of
~19x P/E during 2014-25 with an operating profit CAGR of 20%, implying a PEG of 1.0. We think
our target multiple of 45x is justified by the 61% operating profit CAGR we project for

2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected macro conditions
n Stronger semi and photonics and power testing demand
n Margin improvement via better product mix
n Better opex control
Risks to Downside
n Macro slowdown
n Softer-than-expected AI capex
n Slower progress in the advanced packaging capacity expansion
n Weaker consumer electronics and automobile related demand
n Margin contraction arising from lack of revenue scale and increasing opex

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

Document 155: AI Adoption Tracker_ August 2026_ Adoption Rises to 22.4%

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI Adoption Tracker_ August 2026_ Adoption Rises to 22.4%.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-09-01
Pages: 24
Status: ok

### Page 1

We update our AI adoption tracker through August 2026. n
AI-related investment growth remains strong, particularly for semiconductors, n
where equity analysts expect global revenue to reach $863bn (ann.) by the end 
of 2026. 2026 gross profit forecast revisions since 2022Q3 for AI-exposed global 
public companies currently stands at $1.1tn. AI-related hardware investment in 
the US national accounts also continues to rise and now stands at $485bn 
(3-month annualized average; 1.5% of GDP) above its 2022 level. AI-related 
hardware shipments from Taiwan have grown by 76% since 2022 and US AI 
hardware net imports ticked up to $55.9bn in June.  
AI adoption by firms rose by 0.9pp to 22.4% among US establishments (with n
adoption expected to rise to 25.9% in the next 6 months) according to the 
Census Bureau’s Business Trends and Outlook Survey (BTOS). Information is 
leading sector adoption at nearly 50%. At a detailed subsector level, computing 
and publishing firms continue to lead adoption, and warehousing and 
miscellaneous manufacturing firms report the largest expected increase in 
adoption over the next six months. Establishments with over 250 employees 
continue to lead in adoption (40%; latest six survey average) while adoption has 
grown more slowly recently among smaller firms. Other business surveys note 
that 40% of surveyed organizations have begun deploying and scaling AI agents. 
AI’s labor market impact remains visible but narrow. We continue to observe n
employment drags in specific industries where AI-use cases have been 
established such as marketing, graphic design, customer service, and some tech 
occupations. However, this is offset by construction job growth in data 
center-exposed categories, which is up 209k since 2022 (relative to broader 
construction employment trends). The tech share of total employment continues 
to decline relative to the pre-2022 trend. The correlation between AI adoption 
and broad labor market slack measures remains limited but has strengthened in 
recent months. Corporate layoffs attributed to AI affected 11k employees in 
June, bringing the 2026 YTD total to 113k. 22% of Russell 3000 companies 
mentioned AI and labor-related keywords in 2026Q2. 
We continue to observe large impacts on labor productivity in the limited areas n
where generative AI has been deployed but only modest signs of economy-wide 
impacts. Academic studies imply a 23% average uplift to productivity, while 
company anecdotes imply slightly larger efficiency gains of around 32%. 
Sarah Dong

Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC 
Joseph Briggs

“producing goods and services” to asking about AI use in “any business functions” 
starting from the December 4, 2025 release. There was a level shift observed in 
conjunction with the new question wording, and we will be tracking adoption rates 
under the new methodology going forward.

### Page 11

Exhibit 15: 40% of Surveyed Organizations Have Begun Deploying and Scaling AI Agents 
Source Date Current Adoption / Impacts Future Adoption / Impacts
McKinsey August 2026
40% of large organization respondents have adopted AI agents and are 
scaling their deployment throughout the organization, up from 27% last year. 
Nearly a third of respondents reported that they had used agentic coding 
tools to build software in-house instead of purchasing it. 80% of respondents 
said AI has improved their individual productivity. 
60% of surveyed organizations expect to increase their AI investments in the 
year ahead. 39% of respondents expect AI-related declines in their 
organization's total employment in the coming year. 
Gallup July 2026
47% of surveyed US employees in Q2 said their organization has integrated 
AI tools to improve workplace productivity, up from 41% last quarter. 
Individual AI use at work also increased to 52% of respondents. Among AI 
users, the most common uses of AI are writing and editing (51%), search or 
research (49%), and general assistance or problem-solving (39%).
The highest productivity ratings come from employees using AI for coding 
assistance and automation or process automation. Employees who use AI 
for a greater variety of tasks were also  more likely to report that AI positively 
affects their personal productivity. Although corporate adoption and AI 
awareness has grown notably, one in five employees remain unsure if AI has 
been integrated at their organization
WRITER April 2026
52% of employees say they've used AI agents over the past year. 
Employees reported saving around 6 hours per week with AI tools, while 
executives reported saving twice as much time at nearly 12 hours per week. 
The top use cases include editing and proofreading, personalized 
communication, and data analysis and insights. Few surveyed leaders 
reported seeing significant ROI from generative AI (29%) or AI agents (23%).
69% of executives report doing layoffs due to AI, but 39% admit their 
company doesn't have a formal strategy to drive revenue from AI. 
Harvard Business 
Review and 
Appian
April 2026
Most organizations have moved beyond the experimentation phase with AI 
and 58% of surveyed organizations have AI in production. 69% of 
respondents reported that legacy systems are limiting their organization's 
ability to scale AI. Most respondents are seeing some returns from AI 
investments, but only 16% report realizing a high degree of measurable 
value. 
Many organizations are currently exploring agentic AI systems (under 
consideration by 62% of respondents and currently used by 25%). Although 
nearly all organizations agree that clear guardrails are needed for AI agent 
adoption, fewer than half say their organization has defined such rules.
Gallup April 2026
Half of all surveyed adults reported using AI in their jobs and 27% of 
employees in AI-adopting organizations say that their workplace has 
changed in disruptive ways due to AI in the past year. 65% of adopters said 
that AI has improved their productivity and efficiency, regardless of how often 
they personally use AI at work. 
Eighteen percent of all U.S. employees say it is very or somewhat likely their 
job will be eliminated within the next five years due to AI or automation. 
Among employees working in organizations that have adopted AI, that share 
rises to 23%. However, employees in AI-adopting organizations were more 
likely to report both current expansions and reductions in headcount. 
NVIDIA March 2026
64% of respondents said their organizations are actively using AI in their 
operations. 28% said they're still in the assessment phase, while 8% said 
they're not using AI and have no plans to. Regional adoption among 
respondents was 70% in North America, 65% in EMEA, and 63% in APAC. 
Larger companies (with over 1000 employees) demonstrated broader 
adoption, deployed more use cases, and reported greater ROI. More than 
half of respondents (53%) said improved employee productivity was one of 
the biggest impacts AI had on business operations.
86% of respondents said their AI budgets would increase this year and 
nearly 40% said budgets would increase by 10% or more. Respondents 
reported that workflow and operations and retaining the right expertise to 
scale AI solutions were the key challenges to adoption. 
Deloitte January 2026
In the latest survey, 34% of companies were starting to use AI to deeply 
transform their businesses, 30% were designing key processes around AI, 
and the remaining 37% were using AI at the surface level with little or no 
change to underlying business processes. 84% of companies have not 
redesignated jobs or work tasks around AI capabilities. 25% of surveyed 
leaders reported that AI is having a transformative effect on their 
organizations, more than double from 12% a year ago. 
Survey results showed that insufficient employee skills were the largest 
barrier to integrating AI into existing workflows. Nearly three-quarters of 
surveyed companies expect to be using agentic AI at least moderately in two 
years. 
Gallup December 2025
The percentage of US employees who reported using AI at work at least a 
few times a week increased from 19% in 2025Q2 to 23% in Q3. 
Unsurprisingly, employees working in knowledge-based jobs (such as tech 
or professional services) were more likely to use AI than those in frontline 
positions. Most common use cases include using AI to consolidate 
information or data, idea generation, and automating basic tasks. Users 
most often use AI chatbots and virtual assistants. 
Gallup research has shown that employee AI adoption is strongly associated 
with having greater managerial support for AI and strategic integration of AI 
in their role, indicating that a strategic people-first approach is needed for 
effective AI adoption.

Green shading indicates surveys added since the last update.

Source: Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research

Document 239: Eurozone _ Growth on track despite the turbulence (2026-09-24)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Eurozone _ Growth on track despite the turbulence (2026-09-24).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 2
Status: ok

### Page 1

8
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
GROWTH ON TRACK DESPITE THE TURBULENCE
Growth was stronger than expected in Q2 and is set to strengthen further in the coming quarters. According to our fore-
casts, it will reach 1.5% in 2027 after 1.1% in 2026. This growth is expected to be driven by investment and its knock-on
effect on exports. On an annual average basis, inflation is expected to stand at 3% in 2026 and 2.8% in 2027, peaking at

the end of 2026. The ECB is expected to continue its monetary tightening. At this stage, a single further rate rise, aimed at
keeping inflation expectations anchored, is expected in December. The impact of rising interest rates (key policy rates and
market rates) on public finances and private sector borrowing conditions will be worth monitoring.
GROWTH: BEYOND THE SHOCKS, A RESILIENT MOMENTUM
Growth in the Eurozone remained robust in the first half of 2026, at

0.25% q/q in Q1 and 0.33% q/q in Q2, excluding Ireland (where economic
activity is volatile). It was driven by Germany, where signs of a recovery
are becoming clearer (see Germany). Our baseline scenario forecasts
growth of 1.1% in 2026, followed by 1.5% in 2027 on an annual average
basis. This growth is expected to be driven by the strengthening of
trends already underway in public and private investment (particularly
in defence and AI) and in exports (trade in services and intra-zone trade

in goods). Household consumption is likely to be resilient too. According
to our forecasts, job creation would continue to underpin household
incomes, thereby cushioning the impact of inflation.
A NEW LEASE OF LIFE FOR INDUSTRY
The Eurozone is undergoing one of the most profound industrial trans-
formations in its history. This is giving rise to divergences between key
sectors but, above all, to new drivers of growth whose effects are beco-
ming increasingly apparent (see our analysis). Indeed, the signs of an

economic recovery are growing stronger. Business sentiment indicators
show a marked improvement in the economic outlook across most sec-
tors (Chart 2), driven primarily by electronics (AI, digitalisation), the
automotive sector and aerospace. European industry is thus benefi -
ting from strong demand for capital goods; a structural trend that will
continue to be fuelled by ongoing investment cycles in AI, defence and
electrification. The introduction of minimum European content quotas

in the draft law on the industrial accelerator, together with the Com -
mission’s intention to tighten the criteria for access to European public
procurement markets, will provide additional support for European in-
dustry in the longer term.
FOREIGN TRADE: A REPOSITIONING THAT IS PAYING OFF
Following a decline in 2026, linked to rising energy import costs, the
trade surplus is expected to rise to around 3.0% of GDP in 2027 (of
which 2% of GDP for goods and 1% for services). Growth in European ex-

ports has slowed compared with its pre-Covid trend and is being ham-
pered by the decline in Chinese and US markets. However, Eurozone
exports in volume rose significantly in Q2 (+3.4% q/q).
More importantly, since Liberation Day (April 2025), Eurozone coun -
tries have found significant sources of growth amongst other European
partners, which has enabled them to cushion the decline in exports to
certain third markets. This is evident in exports to the rest of the EU,

EFTA countries (Switzerland in particular) and, to a lesser extent, the
United Kingdom, Ukraine and the Balkans (Chart 3). The momentum of
intra-European trade therefore remains very strong. It is expected to
strengthen further with the anticipated investments in defence, AI and
electrification.
FINANCING: FINANCIAL CONDITIONS ARE EXPECTED TO REMAIN TIGHT
Financial conditions in the Eurozone remained tight in Q2 2026, accor-
ding to indices calculated by the ECB (including the BIG [Broad Inter -

mediation Gauge]). They are expected to tighten more significantly in
the second half of the year: i/ the rise in long-term bond yields, which
has accelerated since the outbreak of the conflict in Iran, is expected to
continue; and ii/ the ECB will continue its monetary tightening (+25 bps

EcoPerspectives / 3rd Quarter 2026 / September 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
The bank
for a changing
world
in Q3, then in Q4). For the time being, the debt burden is still weighing
only moderately on public finances due to the long average maturity of
countries’ debt (8.2 years on average in the Eurozone in July), but this
burden will increase by 2030.
On the corporate front, the rise in interest rates has remained contained
(+2 bps since January 2026, reaching 3.9% in July), which has contri -

buted to the strong growth in new investment loans (cumulated over
one year, +9.1% y/y in July compared with 15.3% y/y in September 2025).
By contrast, cumulative net debt securities issues over one year re -
mained stable in July, whilst cumulative net issues of listed shares over
one year halved month on month.
Rising interest rates will continue to hamper new variable-rate
home loans to households (around 65% of new lending) more signi -
ficantly than fixed-rate loans, for which rates stood in July at 3.69%

(+4 bps m/m) and 3.36% (+2 bps m/m) respectively. New home loans,
which are stable in 2026 on a cumulative over one year basis, are there-
fore unlikely to see any improvement. A fall in real-estate transactions
could weigh on new consumer credit through a reduction in expenditure
on home furnishings and renovations, whereas consumer credit has
been fairly buoyant so far (year-to-date, +5.2% y/y in July compared
with +1.4% y/y in January).
LABOUR MARKET: RESILIENCE YET TO BE CONFIRMED

Employment in the Eurozone rose again by an average of 0.1% q/q in
the first half of 2026, driven by Spain and Portugal. Given the growth
outlook in these two countries, this momentum is likely to continue,
whilst business sentiment surveys in Germany suggest that employ -
ment is beginning to stabilise. Nevertheless, employment levels are
plateauing (France, the Netherlands) or even falling in other countries
(Austria, Finland), which is likely to slow the decline in the Eurozone

unemployment rate, meaning it is expected to remain close to current
levels in 2027. Furthermore, second-round effects of the rise in inflation
on wages cannot be ruled out: the ECB’s wage tracker suggests wage
growth of close to 3% by summer 2027 (2.9% y/y in June 2027, compared
with 2.6% in September).
INFLATION: A PEAK EXPECTED IN LATE 2026
Changes in wage dynamics will be a key factor in the inflation out -
look for 2027, whilst energy price rises are picking up again, driven

by the conflict in the Middle East. This rise is even sharper than in
the spring for gas (linked to restocking ahead of winter) and is likely
to feed through to electricity prices in several countries. We therefore
anticipate inflation peaking at 3.7% y/y in Q4 2026, before easing as the
energy component falls. On an annual average, headline inflation is
expected to stand at 3% in 2026 and then at 2.8% in 2027.
At this stage, the pass-through of rising energy prices to other com -

ponents of the consumer price index, particularly services, remains li -
mited. The alternative core indicators monitored by the ECB, including
the PCCI (Persistent and Common Component of Inflation, 2.1% in July),
remain close to the 2% target. Core inflation, however, is expected to
peak two months later than headline inflation (3% in March 2027).
Inflationary risks are nevertheless tilted to the upside. These are lin -
ked to food prices, which are expected to be affected by the energy

shock and weather conditions (summer drought, El Niño); they could
also stem from a delayed impact of energy price rises on underlying
inflation. The rise in prices of energy-dependent inputs (notably che -
micals and plastics) and those linked to the AI sector is expected to be
sustained. Growth, which we expect to remain robust over the coming
quarters, will also fuel underlying inflationary pressures.
MONETARY POLICY: TIGHTENING WITHOUT SLOWING (GROWTH)

Following two 25-basis-point rises (in June and September), we ex -
pect a third in December, which would bring the deposit facility rate
to 2.75%. It would then be in slightly restrictive territory (we estimate
the upper bound of the neutral rate to be 2.5%). This rise would be
aimed primarily at anchoring inflation expectations and, consequently,
at curbing the pass-through effects of inflation. Monetary tightening
would therefore remain limited. Indeed, the overshoot of the inflation

target would be temporary, and the operational framework adopted by
the ECB in June 2025 allows it to tolerate this, provided it remains brief.
However, it cannot be ruled out that the ECB might take further action
if the decline in inflation proves slower than expected.
PUBLIC FINANCES ARE CONTINGENT ON RISING INTEREST RATES
The fiscal impulse in the Eurozone is expected to be clearly positive in
2026 due to the rise in defence and infrastructure spending in Germany.

This will contribute to a widening of the country’s primary deficit (from
+0.9 pp of GDP to 2.8%, according to our forecasts). The improvement in
public finances is expected to stall in both France and Italy, at least in
2026, due to the impact of inflation on the debt burden via index-linked
bonds. This trend is expected to reverse in 2027 with less expansionary
fiscal policies. Nevertheless, the increase in the debt service burden
would fuel a further rise in the public deficit, which would thus reach

around 3.3% and 3.5% of GDP in 2026 and 2027 respectively (compared
with 2.9% in 2025). Against this backdrop, the Eurozone’s public debt-
to-GDP ratio is projected to exceed 90% of GDP in 2026 and reach 93%
of GDP in 2027, according to our forecasts.
Article completed on 15 September 2026

Document 252: France’s 2027 budget is only the first fiscal test

Source PDF: all report pdf/France’s 2027 budget is only the first fiscal test.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

France’s 2027 budget would prevent a sharp deterioration in
the public ﬁnances, but it would not be enough to stabilise
public debt. Its adoption would provide some relief, although
ﬁscal and political risks are likely to remain well beyond next
year's presidential election
Charlotte de Montpellier
The Ministry of the Economy and Finance in Bercy, Paris. Even if the current
budget proposal is adopted, ﬁscal uncertainty will not disappear in France

France is in the eye of the storm. Its public ﬁnances have
deteriorated signiﬁcantly, the budget faces a diﬃcult
parliamentary process, and the presidential election oﬀers no clear
prospect of political stabilisation. Economic conditions provide little
support; we expect GDP growth of only 0.3% in 2026 and 0.6% in
2027.
France’s debt dynamics are particularly unfavourable. Over the past
15 years, its public debt ratio has risen signiﬁcantly, while many

other European countries have reduced theirs. French public debt
reached 119% of GDP in mid-2026 and is expected to increase to
Cookie settings

### Page 2

121.7% in 2027. Persistent primary deﬁcits, rising interest
payments and weak growth mean that debt will continue to rise
without sustained ﬁscal adjustment.
The budget would limit the deterioration,
not stabilise debt
The draft budget aims to reduce the public deﬁcit to 5% of GDP in
2027, after 5.4% in 2026 and 5.1% in 2025. This requires €43bn of
new measures. Without them, the government estimates that the
deﬁcit would reach around 6.5% next year.

This is a substantial adjustment and is consistent with France’s
European expenditure trajectory. But a 5% deﬁcit remains too high
to stabilise debt, particularly with weak growth and a rising interest
bill. Even if implemented in full, the budget would only limit the
deterioration; the debt ratio would continue to rise.
The budget is likely to pass, but concessions
may be needed
The parliamentary process can run until 17 December under the
70-day constitutional timetable. Without a majority in the National

Assembly, the government is unlikely to secure approval in a
normal vote. Our base case is that it uses Article 49.3 to adopt the
budget without one, potentially after making concessions to
ensure that a subsequent no-conﬁdence motion fails. This could
reduce the ﬁnal ﬁscal adjustment.
We, nevertheless, expect the government to survive. Opposition
parties have little incentive to trigger another political crisis shortly
before the presidential election, take responsibility for the resulting

instability and risk a further deterioration in the public ﬁnances.
Alternatively, the government could let the deadline expire and
impose its original budget by ordinance. It would ﬁrst have to
survive any pre-emptive no-conﬁdence motion. Once the
ordinances were adopted, however, the budget would remain in
force even if the government subsequently fell.

Public ﬁnances GDP France Eurozone Elections
Failing either outcome, a special law would ensure continuity of the
state. But the deadlock could last until the summer and, without
corrective measures, cause a sharp deterioration in the public
ﬁnances.
France therefore faces another two months of budget uncertainty.
Adoption would be a relative success, preventing the deﬁcit from
rising towards 6.5%. But concessions could dilute the adjustment,
while even the original package would not stabilise debt.

Fiscal uncertainty will persist in 2027
Even if the budget is adopted, ﬁscal uncertainty will not disappear.
The next government will still have to implement further reforms.
Yet the presidential election on 18 April and 2 May 2027 will
probably be followed by legislative elections, which could again
produce a fragmented National Assembly and make those reforms
diﬃcult to pass.
There is also little clarity on the future ﬁscal strategy. Few
prospective candidates have presented suﬃciently detailed plans

to stabilise public ﬁnances, and almost none appear willing to
tackle pensions, despite their being the largest component of public
spending and central to France’s structural ﬁscal problem.
The National Rally, which leads ﬁrst-round polls, has made ﬁscal
consolidation a priority. But its plan, in our view, relies on very
optimistic macroeconomic assumptions and diﬃcult-to-deliver
savings, as discussed in our assessment of its ﬁscal programme.
The far-left La France Insoumise and its candidate, Jean-Luc

Mélenchon, support cancelling part of the debt held by the central
bank and pursuing an expansionary ﬁscal policy. There is even less
visibility on who will emerge from the centre, the right and the rest
of the left, or on their ﬁscal plans.
The 2027 budget will be a critical ﬁrst step in containing France’s
ﬁscal deterioration. Whether that adjustment can be sustained and
extended after the election remains much more uncertain.

策。
• ⾮票委鷹派：Alberto Musalem 主張  6–9 個⽉內再升、 Jeff Schmid 直⾔  AI 已是主要通膨推⼿、 Austan
Goolsbee 形容通膨久⾼「是在玩⽕」，⾮票委整體⽐票委更鷹；但他們今年沒有投票權，影響在於⽀撐  12
⽉與  2027 年初的升息定價，⽽⾮  10 ⽉的決策。
• 結論：市場已跟著票委的耐⼼訊號轉向。 CME 的  10 ⽉升息機率從  9/28 的  70.9% 降⾄  10/9 的  17.7% ，按兵
不動升⾄  82.3% ；但  12 ⽉累計升息機率仍有  82.7% ， 9 ⽉會議紀要也顯⽰多數官員認為年底前再升⼀次合

適。 10 ⽉暫停是延後，不是升息循環結束。

結論
數據更新⾄  2026-10-09 。
四⼤結論
1  10 ⽉按兵不動是基準情境， 12 ⽉再升⼀碼才是主路徑。 9 ⽉⾮農僅增  2.9 萬、失業率升⾄
4.2% 、時薪年增放緩⾄  3.0% ，給了  John Williams 、 Philip Jefferson 等票委等待的理由；但  9 ⽉會
議紀要顯⽰多數與會者認為年底前再升⼀次合適， Christopher Waller 也只是把升息的時點交給數
據決定。截⾄  10/9 ， CME 顯⽰  10 ⽉不動機率  82.3% 、 12 ⽉累計升息機率  82.7% 。投資上不宜為
降息預期定價， 3 個⽉期國庫券殖利率  4.25% 仍是等待資⾦的最佳停泊處，成⻑股的評價要靠盈
餘與現⾦流⽀撐，⽽⾮寬鬆預期。
2   暫停預期隨時可能逆轉，關鍵在油價與核⼼服務通膨。 10/9 布蘭特油價收在每桶  104.72 美元，

9 ⽉  ISM 服務業物價指數升⾄  74.0 、為  2022 年  7 ⽉以來新⾼，製造業物價指數  77.9 ，紐約  Fed
調查的⼀年期通膨預期升⾄  3.9% ，四項都指向通膨的第⼆輪效應。 10/14 公布的  9 ⽉  CPI 若核⼼
服務⾛強， 12 ⽉升息將被坐實、 10 ⽉升息機率也可能回升，這呼應  Lisa Cook 、 Philip Jefferson
對通膨擴散的警告。反向的條件是⾮農、零售與初領失業⾦同步轉弱，屆時衰退⾵險會取代通膨
⾵險，政策路徑才會轉向。
3   ⻑天期美債仍不宜承接，短天期國庫券是較佳的等待資⾦。 10 年期美債殖利率  10/5 收在
5.31% 、 10/7 盤中觸及約  5.36% 創  2002 年以來新⾼， 10/9 收在  5.24% ；同期  2 年期由  9/28 的
4.92% 回落⾄  4.80% ，顯⽰⻑端壓⼒主要來⾃期限溢價、財政與  AI 融資供給，⽽⾮政策路徑。
Alberto Musalem 估計  AI 帶動的資本需求約占  GDP 3%–4% 且將持續  5–10 年， Beth Hammack 更

直⾔殖利率上升無法取代升息。要等核⼼  PCE 連續降溫、⻑端殖利率不再創⾼、標售需求改善三
者同時出現，才是分批拉⻑存續期間的時機。
4   股票維持核⼼部位，但加碼⾨檻要提⾼；⿈⾦與美元各有分⼯。經濟仍在擴張、官員多認為勞
動市場撐得住升息，股票仍優於⻑債，但美股與  AI 供應鏈的加碼應以⾃由現⾦流、未履約訂單
（ RPO ）與資本報酬率驗證，避開依賴遠期現⾦流的⾼估值部位。⿈⾦  10/9 收在每盎司  4,216 美
元，適合維持  10%–15% 的分批避險部位，承擔財政與地緣⾵險，實質利率急升時暫緩加碼。美
元指數  10/9 為  102.21 、接近  10/5 創下的  52 週⾼點  102.54 ，保留美元短券作為流動性即可，不宜
因官員偏鷹就單邊押注美元⾛強。

Document 306: Q3 Job Vacancies_ another step lower (2026-10-15)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Q3 Job Vacancies_ another step lower (2026-10-15).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 12
Status: ok

### Page 1

#labour #labour #employment
Q3 Job Vacancies: another step lower
By Neha Sharma
Economist, Westpac
13:07 October 01 2026 Share
The number of job vacancies fell by 3.0k (–0.9%qtr) between May and August. The decline
was wholly driven by the private sector, where vacancies fell 2.0%qtr. In contrast, public
sector vacancies rose 7.8%qtr.

### Page 2

Job vacancies fell by 3.0k (–0.9%qtr) between May and August, extending the previous quarter’s
decline. The May and February estimates were also revised slightly lower. As a result, vacancies are
now 1.4% below their level a year earlier. The result was at odds with partial indicators, which all
pointed to a rise in the quarter.

The decline was wholly driven by the private sector, where vacancies fell 2.0%qtr to be 1.5%yr lower.
In contrast, public sector vacancies rose 7.8%qtr, marking the strongest quarterly increase in four years
and partially reversing the sharp 7.5%qtr decline recorded in the May quarter. Public sector vacancies
were flat over the year.

Job vacancies have largely moved sideways since early 2025, though a resumption of the broader
downtrend now appears increasingly likely. Labour demand has held up reasonably well, though not
sufficiently to match growth in labour supply, resulting in a gradual easing in labour market conditions
(see here). Today’s result is also consistent with our view that this process will continue into early 2027
as the cumulative effects of high inflation and higher interest rates continue to weigh on activity and
hiring demand.

### Page 3

The vacancy-to-unemployment ratio, a key measure of labour market tightness closely monitored by
the RBA, declined from 0.48 to 0.45. The fall was the sharpest since early 2025, reflecting both a
decline in vacancies and a 6.8%qtr increase in the number of unemployed people over the quarter,
largely owing to the continued strength in labour supply. The ratio remains well above its long-run
average of around 0.2.

Given the strong growth in labour supply, the Beveridge curve provides a useful framework for
assessing recent labour market developments. By comparing vacancies and unemployment as shares
of the labour force, it controls for growth in the overall size of the labour market. Between 2022 and
2024, the labour market moved down the steep portion of the Beveridge curve, with substantial
declines in vacancies occurring alongside only modest increases in unemployment. While labour
market tightness has continued to unwind, Australia remains in this region of the curve, suggesting that
further rebalancing between labour demand and supply can occur through lower vacancies without a
material rise in unemployment.

### Page 4

All statistics mentioned below are not seasonally adjusted and hence may not add up to the seasonally
adjusted statistics referenced above.

By industry, job vacancies declined in eight of the 18 industries over the three months to August. The
largest falls were recorded in wholesale trade (–18.2%qtr), professional, scientific & technical services
(–17.4%qtr), and information media & telecommunications (–16.7%qtr). Consumer-facing sectors were
mixed, with retail trade (24.6%) and arts & recreation services (10.3%) recording strong increases,
while accommodation & food services edged 0.9% lower. Only three industries have seen vacancies
fall below their pre-pandemic levels: administrative & support services (–24.3%qtr), information media
& telecommunications (–9.1%qtr), and financial & insurance services (–7.3%qtr).

Overall market sector vacancies were flat in the quarter, meanwhile the non-market sector saw a 2.9%
drop, owing to declines in education & training (–8.2%qtr) and health care & social assistance (–
4.2%qtr). For detailed insights on industry condition, see Q2 Labour Account and the Westpac
Business Signal.

interposer production to GFS;

因矽中介層產能不⾜，台積電⾸次合作外包給競爭者 GFS ，這應該算
台積電的主動外溢訂單
ANDREW LU ON GLOBAL SEMI/TECHS
OCT 9 ∙ PAID

READ IN APP
What is new?
半導體晶圓代⼯領域誕⽣了⼀項具備歷史分⽔嶺意義的競合⼤案，全球晶圓代
⼯⿓頭台積電（ TSMC ）與⻑期競爭對⼿美商格芯（ GlobalFoundries, GFS ）
正式簽署⼀項為期五年、價值  20 億美元， 2028 上半年開始量產的製造供應
協議。根據合約，格芯將在其位於美國紐約州⾺爾他（ Malta, NY ）的旗艦
Fab 8 晶圓廠，專⾨為台積電的  CoWoS 先進封裝⽣態系⽣產最關鍵的「矽中
介層（ Silicon Interposers ）」。 Silicon interposer 做完 RDL 層後與邏輯晶⽚
與記憶體進⾏接合，對⾼頻寬、訊號完整性與封裝良率⾄關重要；它不是最先
進  GPU 邏輯  die ，也不代表  GlobalFoundries 取得  Nvidia ∕ AMD 的先進
節點代⼯。
格芯因此成為全美國境內第⼀座具備量產能⼒、專為先進封裝供貨的本⼟矽中
介層供應基地，徹底解決了台積電亞利桑那鳳凰城  Fab 21 在美⽣產

4nm/3nm/2nm 裸晶後，因美國缺乏先進矽中介層製造⽽被迫將晶圓空運回
台灣封裝或將矽中介層空運到美國的荒謬地緣斷鏈難題。

### Page 2

⾵險即可跨⼊⾼⽑利的  AI 先進封裝關鍵環節。但要是台積電在 2030 年⽤玻璃
中介層替代矽中介層轉進  CoPoS ，這訂單上修的機會將被移除。

Wafer ）。

Negatively aﬀect what supply chain vendors and
why?
台灣⼆線矽中介層代⼯廠（聯電  2303.TW 、世界先進  5347.TW ）：台積電
過去在台灣將部分  CoWoS-S 矽中介層外包給聯電等本⼟夥伴；隨著美系客戶
指定美國在地化⽣產，聯電爭取海外  CoWoS 衍⽣中介層訂單的空間遭到分
流，美國第⼆來源降低稀缺溢價並提⾼客戶議價權。
Our outside-the-box, non-consensus thinking
台積電把關鍵的  CoWoS 中介層交給技術競爭對⼿，是否引狼⼊室？我們的⾮

共識觀點指出，矽中介層本質上是⾼耗⾯積、低電晶體密度的低⽑利率材料。
矽中介層通常使⽤  65nm 甚⾄  90nm ⽼舊製程製造，上⾯幾乎沒有主動電晶
體，純粹是⽤深矽穿孔（ TSV ）進⾏⾼密度⾦屬布線  RDL ，但它卻要霸佔珍貴
的  12 吋潔淨室機台空間。台積電將這種「吃產能、賺⼯錢」的低⽑利環節甩
給急需產能利⽤率的格芯，⾃⼰則全⼒聚焦在單⽚要價  3 萬美元的
3nm/2nm 核⼼運算晶粒與頂層⾼難度鍵合。台積電不是出讓技術，⽽是進⾏
⾼超的微笑曲線資本利潤套利。
⽽且這筆交易的真正戰略價值不僅僅是美國製造，⽽是把先進封裝拆成可外包
的模組。若  TSMC 能標準化  interposer PDK 、測試與良率資料，未來可像前
段  foundry 的多廠調度⼀樣管理封裝供應鏈；這會提⾼總體產能韌性，但也
把競爭焦點轉到跨廠協同、良率  ownership 與責任界⾯。⻑期勝者可能不是
單⼀設備商，⽽是能把  design-to-package 數位線索串起來的  EDA ∕

manufacturing data 平台。
Our advice to investors
我們不該把協議解讀為  TSMC 技術外流或  GlobalFoundries 進⼊最先進邏輯
製程。應繼續追蹤四項指標： 1. 1H28 是否能實際量產、 2. GFS 的  silicon
interposer 良率與產能、 3. TSMC 內部  CoWoS 瓶頸釋放幅度、 4. 是否追加
訂單。 US2bn 若平均分五年為 US400mn ∕年，但估值模型應按  2028 起的爬
坡曲線認列。
這項世紀⼤和解是台積電進⾏地緣政治壓⼒卸載的操作。台積電不僅沒有因此
喪失封裝主導權，反⽽⽤外包中介層的代價，將原本對⽴的格芯轉化為⾃⾝的
利益同盟，並在美國本⼟牢牢築起  CoWoS ⽣態圈。我們維持對台積電⻑期的
正⾯評價並同時看好格芯（ GFS ）的結構性轉變。

Document 358: USA_ Pushing Back Fed Rate Hike on Lower Core PCE Inflation; Q2 GDP Revised Up to +2.2%; Trade Deficit Widens More Than Expected; Lowering Q3 GDP Tracking to +3.3%

Source PDF: all report pdf/USA_ Pushing Back Fed Rate Hike on Lower Core PCE Inflation; Q2 GDP Revised Up to +2.2%; Trade Deficit Widens More Than Expected; Lowering Q3 GDP Tracking to +3.3%.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 8

Personal consumption (third estimate) +3.8% (qoq ar) for Q2 vs. GS +3.4%, median

MAIN POINTS:
1. The core PCE index increased +0.25% in August, somewhat below expectations, and
the year-over-year rate increased 3.01%. We estimate that the Dallas Fed’s trimmed
mean PCE measure increased 0.16% month over month and 2.20% year over year.
2. Year-over-year core PCE was revised downward 0.36pp in July to 2.98%, primarily
reflecting the methodological revisions to the calculation of the portfolio management
(-0.23pp), computer accessories and software (-0.11pp), and legal services (+0.01pp)

components of PCE. These downward revisions were larger than we expected, mostly
reflecting a larger revision to the portfolio management component than we expected.
3. Today’s inflation report, especially the downward revisions resulting from the
methodological changes, imply that core PCE inflation is likely to substantially
undershoot the median FOMC participant’s forecast of 3.4% for 2026 on a Q4/Q4 basis.
We now expect a 3.0% increase for core PCE in 2026 on a Q4/Q4 basis. Coupled with

comments from New York Fed President John Williams yesterday that “with the policy
action we took at our September meeting, there is no need for urgency, and we have
time to gather more information,” we now think that an October hike is unlikely.  We are
pushing back the second hike in our forecast to December, and we see a strong chance
that the FOMC will ultimately conclude that additional rate hikes are unnecessary.
4. Real GDP was revised up by 0.7pp to +2.2% in Q2 (quarter-over-quarter annualized),

largely reflecting upward revisions to consumption (+0.4pp to +3.8%) and investment
(+1.9pp to +4.6%) growth. The strength in consumer spending in Q2 likely continued to
reflect the boost to consumer cashflow from larger-than-usual tax refunds, but we
continue to expect consumer spending growth to slow somewhat in 2026Q4 as the fiscal
support fades and the headwind from higher energy prices remains. Stronger
investment growth reflected an upward revision to private fixed investment (+0.5pp to

+9.0%), driven by data center investment in the nonresidential structures category
(+1.9pp to +0.1%), and an upward revision to the contribution from inventories (+0.2pp
to -0.5pp).
5. Reflecting today’s annual revisions to the national accounts, real GDP was revised up
by 0.4pp to +2.5% in Q1 (quarter-over-quarter annualized), largely reflecting upward
revisions to the contributions from consumption and exports. Revisions for prior years
were modest: real GDP growth on a Q4/Q4 basis was unrevised for 2025 (+2.0%) and

2023 (+3.4%), and slightly revised down for 2024 (-0.2pp to +2.4%) and up for 2022
30 September 2026   2
Goldman Sachs

### Page 3

(+0.1pp to +1.3%). 
6. Personal income increased 0.2% in August, below expectations. Year-over-year 
disposable personal income growth reached 4.8% in August. Personal spending rose 
0.9% in August, in line with consensus expectations. The savings rate decreased 0.5pp to 
4.1% in August, after a large 1.6pp upward revision to the savings rate last month driven 
by an upward revision to the level of disposable personal income (+1.2pp contribution) 
and a downward revision to personal spending as a share of DPI (+0.4pp). 
7. According to the Advance Economic Indicators Report, the goods trade deficit 
widened by $13.7bn to $132.6bn (seasonally adjusted) in August, well above 
expectations. Goods exports increased by $3.7bn to $203.4bn, largely driven by an 
increase in industrial supplies exports. Goods imports increased by $17.4bn to 
$336.1bn, driven by a large increase in industrial supplies and capital goods imports. 
8. According to the ADP report, private sector employment rose by 90k in September 
(month-over-month, seasonally adjusted), slightly above consensus expectations. 
Employment in the services sector rose 59k, primarily reflecting a 55k increase in 
education and health services and a 22k increase in leisure and hospitality employment 
that were partly offset by a 16k decrease in financial activities employment. 
Employment in goods-producing industries increased by 31k, driven by a 17k increase in 
manufacturing and a 15k increase in construction employment. ADP’s new median 
annual base pay measure, which better tracks other measures of wage growth than its 
gross pay measure, increased 3.0% for job stayers, unchanged from the August pace, 
and 4.8% for job changers, 0.1pp above the August pace. We left our forecast for August 
nonfarm payroll growth unchanged at +80k (vs. +90k consensus) ahead of Friday’s 
release. 
9. The details of the personal income and spending report were firmer than our previous 
GDP tracking assumptions. While the trade-related details of the Advance Economic 
Indicators report were much weaker than our previous assumptions, suggesting notably 
stronger imports growth in Q3 than we previously assumed, the inventories details were 
significantly stronger. On net, we lowered our Q3 GDP tracking estimate by 0.1pp to 
+3.3% (quarter-over-quarter annualized).

Will Nvidia use Intel Foveros
3D to replace TSMC’s CoWoS?
Passive overﬂow order Part II
Nvidia 要是真的開始成功地採⽤  Intel 的先進封裝  Foveros 3D 來取
代台積電的  CoWoS ，主動式外溢單變成被動式外溢單，⼤客戶將逐

步擴⼤第⼆來源  CoWoS like 產能的⽐重
ANDREW LU ON GLOBAL SEMI/TECHS
OCT 9 ∙ PAID
READ IN APP
What’s new?
GF Securities 分析師  Jeﬀ Pu 於  10 ⽉  9 ⽇發布最新機構研究報告指出，
Nvidia （ NVDA ）正積極評估於  2028 年下半年，將部分次世代  AI 加速器
（對應  Feynman 系列或  Rubin Ultra 延伸架構）之後段先進封裝導⼊  Intel
Foundry 的  Foveros 3D 堆疊技術。值得注意的是，前段  GPU 運算核⼼  Die
仍將  100% 由台積電（ 2330.TW ）先進製程（ 2nm / A16 ）代⼯製造，但後
段  3D 晶⽚整合與中介層封裝將引⼊  Intel 作為第⼆供應商，以解決台積電
CoWoS 產能⻑期吃緊瓶頸。但⻑期還是要看  Intel 在 EMIB/Foveros 先進封
測的良率表現及其成本競爭⼒。

Positive impacts to Intel and supply chain vendors

### Page 2

Intel （ INTC.US ）：若順利獲  Nvidia 採⽤  Foveros ，將是  Intel Foundry
Services （ IFS ）在先進封裝領域具指標性的外部客戶突破，驗證其「封裝優
先於晶圓代⼯」商業策略，有助於填補新墨⻄哥州  Fab 9 及奧勒岡州先進封裝
產能，改善代⼯部⾨虧損，未來再透過  Foveros 合作量產經驗，擴展延伸到
前段晶圓代⼯訂單。這項合作案如果成真，也將對⾕歌跟 Intel EMIB 合作案的
繼續擴⼤有幫助。
